# EasyEDA Pro Agent engineering record

This file is the durable engineering record and common ground for every human or agent working in this repository. Keep it current whenever a requirement, assumption, interface, compatibility tuple, safety boundary, or test result changes. Do not silently turn an open question into a requirement.

Last updated: 2026-10-15 (Europe/Berlin)

## Current checkpoint

### In development: retained current symbol relaunch

Continuation remains on feature/current-symbol-runtime-witness in draft PR36.
The original completed baseline now has an internal one-shot relaunch path.
It requires the original disconnected gateway session, ended dispatch scope,
unchanged checkpoint receipt/source lineage and genuine termination authority.
The intent is journaled before launch. The actual new launch return and its
issued native clock are retained; no old owner or closure proof is checked
after launch invalidates that generation. The old capture is released once
through its original engine, after a separate durable intent. The runtime and
new Job remain owned; runtime.release is never called. Unavailable native clock
data is reported, not fabricated or retried. Launch/release ambiguity and
publication drift consume the attempt while preserving acquired evidence and
foreign journal history. Final clock validation is followed by a journal check.

The opening transfer now reserves the runtime for its entire lifetime, not
only the old Job. A new Job or null launch cannot reopen ordinary navigation
or allow a sibling coordinator to take over. No public schema or MCP route
has been added. Authenticated new-session admission, opening-effect proof,
backend actor/clock, writer/inverse and a disposable complete lifecycle remain
unfinished. This internal checkpoint is not an installed release.

Source research in docs/current-reopen-effects-research-2026-10-15.md identifies
cache replay, asynchronous project startup and detached PCB Save paths. The
next opening profile should be project-only for metadata, with both target
upload caches absent before opening and independently established no-restore,
current-version/nonempty-project premises. It must still qualify relevant
startup producers; a resolved open promise or repeated stable snapshots do not
do that. Backend request-user identity remains distinct from observed UI actors.
No native semantics or persistence guards were weakened.

The 36 focused tests pass with no failures or skips. They exercise real managed
journals, engine/native proof issuers, checkpoint/copy readers and SQL with
synthetic native frames/private owner seams, not a connected editor. Full
types and whole-tree zero-warning type-aware lint pass. Build/sanitizer,
compatibility, package/privacy/repository, 774-method coverage and all seven
official skill checks pass. Rebuilt source352 SHA99bae0e3/runtime3 SHA3ebba5ec
change only facade projections; external pins stay unchanged. Independent
runtime review accepts the transition; final payload CI remains pending.

### In development: owned current symbol closed baseline

Continuation remains on feature/current-symbol-runtime-witness in draft PR36.
The current opener now reserves a baseline operation and its retained Job once,
collects a fresh owned metadata preparation, and transfers that original
generation to a separate private owner. The old active-generation validator is
not relaxed. Re-preparation and non-inspect opening actions on the reserved Job
refuse, including from another coordinator sharing the same runtime. A reused
baseline ID cannot replace a retained coordinator. Already-issued sibling
generations and ordinary continuations also recheck that reservation after
awaits; only the baseline's private generation can use its reserved Job.

The internal baseline coordinator captures process authority inside the original
authenticated scope, retains the actual capture before further checks, ends the
scope, observes the owned window and records one normal-close intent before
dispatch. It retains the close acknowledgment before checking or publishing it.
Only a genuinely issued pending termination observation permits another probe;
normal close and copy cannot repeat. Conflicting journal changes refuse without
overwriting the changed history. Capture root/Job and original launch projection
are independently correlated. Pending observations and completed-copy authority
are rechecked after publication awaits. A stopped-record failure retains both
the primary failure and the recording error.

The separate copy stage uses the genuine original owner and termination proof.
It creates a new checkpoint at the post-opening closed hash, retains a raw byte
copy, reads the complete SQL inventory, and prepares metadata from the new
checkpoint against the fresh closed-target observation. It verifies the exact
project/branch/owner, undeleted active PCB, target symbol, checkpoint/copy file
lineage and receipt, including the original source device/inode/birth time.
All acquired evidence is retained before follow-up checks.
The VACUUM checkpoint is not substituted for the raw full-SQL baseline.

This is internal source implementation only. No MCP writer route or new public
schema is enabled. Opening effects, before-write baseline, backend actor/clock,
writer/inverse and complete disposable lifecycle remain unqualified. Captures
remain retained; no automatic release, relaunch, Save/Discard selection or
recovery from lost handles is added. Independent review accepts the final

## Non-negotiable safety rules

1. User authority defines scope. Inspection never implies permission to edit, save, discard unsaved state, restore a database, fabricate, purchase, publish a release, or contact a third party.
2. Never mutate a real design to test a writer. Connected writer validation must use a disposable, explicitly identified sacrificial project and must prove exact live state, collateral invariants, save/close/reopen state, logical SQLite change, and rollback.
3. Never enable the private writer merely by setting a flag in production. Record the connected evidence, review it, update the capability docs, add regression tests, and make the enablement change deliberately.
4. Never bypass the global bridge quarantine. An orphan-risk or unreadable journal blocks every live bridge path except the isolated nonce-bound recovery resolver. Local discovery and managed artifact/evidence inspection may continue.
5. Never replay a restart challenge. Legacy recovery retains its user-attestation contract. Typed recovery uses an internal operation-bound technical nonce plus retained old-process termination and replacement authenticated-session evidence; it does not require a new user message copying that nonce or authorize discard/force termination.
6. Never save after an ambiguous call, invariant mismatch, context drift, runtime drift, or durable-baseline drift.
7. Preserve unrelated user changes. Do not rewrite the upstream `easyeda-mcp-pro` checkout as part of facade work.
8. Never commit credentials, private keys, EasyEDA projects, generated evidence, operation journals, or user-specific design data.

## Engineering workflow

- Read the relevant skill and reference document before changing a capability.
- Prefer narrow typed schemas, facade-generated bridge programs, exact independent readers, explicit collateral invariants, and complete inverse models.
- Every awaited bridge boundary needs context reproof appropriate to its risk.
- Journal before dispatch, not after it. Any potentially delayed call needs an orphan marker and recovery path.
- Reject missing, malformed, non-finite, coerced, truncated, or contradictory evidence. Do not turn adapter defects into global corrections.
- Use append-only or exclusive evidence writes, bounded symlink-safe reads, file-descriptor identity checks, and durable directory synchronization.
- Add adversarial tests for every safety fix. A test must fail on the unsafe implementation and prove that no downstream mutation was dispatched.
- Run facade tests through `npm test`, or include `--import ./scripts/test-isolation.ts`
  for a focused Node test invocation. The builtin-only preload allocates a private
  root before static imports and binds control, credential and upstream paths.
  Never point a test at live control data. Retained temporary roots aid failure
  inspection; do not broadly delete them or alter real operation journals.
- Keep source and committed bundle in sync. Do not hand-edit `server/dist/server.mjs`.
- Keep `server/native/easyeda-fd-sanitizer.S`, its linker script, the committed binary, and `server/src/descriptor-sanitizer-identity.ts` synchronized. The exact sanitizer identity is a compatibility boundary, not generated metadata to refresh automatically.

## Required verification

Run on Linux x86_64 with Node exactly `24.18.0`, npm exactly `11.16.0`, and a soft `RLIMIT_CORE` of zero from `plugins/easyeda-pro-control`:

```bash
ulimit -c 0
test "$(ulimit -c)" = "0"
test "$(node --version)" = "v24.18.0"
test "$(npm --version)" = "11.16.0"
npm ci
npm ls --all
npm audit signatures
npm audit --audit-level=moderate
npm run sanitizer:check
npm run verify
```

Then run from the repository root:

```bash
npm --prefix plugins/easyeda-pro-control run repository:validate
for packaged_skill in plugins/easyeda-pro-control/skills/*; do
  python3 "$CODEX_HOME/skills/.system/skill-creator/scripts/quick_validate.py" "$packaged_skill"
done
git diff --exit-code -- plugins/easyeda-pro-control/server/dist/server.mjs plugins/easyeda-pro-control/server/dist/upstream-supervisor.mjs
```

The offline doctor validates the pinned local workstation and is not a substitute for a connected EasyEDA smoke test. Never run a connected mutation smoke test against a user project.

Use the actual installed skill-creator script location if it differs. Run the
official plugin validator when installed; this session may not provide the
historical plugin-creator skill. Repository, MCP-contract and all-skill checks
remain mandatory, not optional because an external helper is absent.

## Compatibility manifest

`plugins/easyeda-pro-control/reviewed-compatibility.json` is a drift gate, not a version wish list. Its launcher fingerprint includes the exact descriptor-sanitizer schema and SHA-256. Update it only after source, sanitizer, and bundle are frozen:

```bash
npm run compatibility:update
npm run compatibility:check
```

The update command changes only the facade source/bundle projections and review timestamp. Upstream, EasyEDA, bridge, tool-catalog, dispatcher, and installed-bundle values require separate evidence and must not be inferred or silently refreshed.

The native boundary requires Linux x86_64 on Linux 5.9 or newer,
`close_range(2)`, `execveat(2)`, and an unprivileged readable
`security.capability` xattr namespace. The sanitizer requires descriptor `10` to
be a regular mode-`0755` file whose capability xattr is absent, establishes
`PR_SET_PDEATHSIG(SIGKILL)` against the unchanged expected facade parent, then
closes every descriptor from `11` upward. GNU binutils `/usr/bin/as` and
`/usr/bin/ld` are needed only to rebuild and verify the source; installed
runtime does not invoke them.

## Release discipline

- Marketplace, plugin manifest, README, skill frontmatter, agent metadata, and MCP tool descriptions must agree about enabled capabilities.
- CI must be green and the built bundle reproducible before publishing.
- Scan the staged tree for secrets and user design artifacts.
- Run the official marketplace installer with umask022. Open private evidence
  outputs under077 separately, then set022 only for the installer subprocess.
  Verify exact committed file modes as well as bytes after installation; a
  successful CLI result alone does not prove executable permissions. A same-HEAD
  marketplace upgrade may be a no-op and must not be reported as a repair.
- Record the test count, commit, marketplace URL, and local installation result in this file and the originating project record.
- Use a new Codex task after installing a new marketplace snapshot.


## Open questions

- Should future releases bundle or bootstrap the upstream MCP, or continue to pin an externally installed checkout?
- Which additional EasyEDA/platform tuples should receive a separate implementation review and disposable-fixture admission?
- Which remaining native-dialog workflows, including ECO and operation-specific manufacturing exports, should gain dedicated typed adapters next? Existing native controls do not imply complete API coverage or fabrication authorization.
